### 3.5.18 Cancel Checkout

#### 3.5.18.1 Description

The Cancel Checkout use case enables the customer to terminate the active checkout process before an order is successfully placed. The customer may voluntarily cancel the checkout at any stage prior to final order creation. Upon cancellation, the system shall terminate the active checkout session, release all temporary resources, discard any uncommitted checkout data, and restore the customer's shopping cart for future use, subject to organizational policies.

If applicable, the system shall release reserved inventory, invalidate temporary payment authorizations, release delivery slot reservations, remove temporary coupon reservations, unlock the checkout session, and restore wallet balances that were only provisionally reserved during checkout. The cancellation process shall ensure that no order is created, no payment is captured, and no permanent business transaction is committed.

The system shall record the cancellation event for auditing, analytics, fraud monitoring, and operational reporting. Customers shall receive confirmation that the checkout has been successfully cancelled and may resume shopping or initiate a new checkout session at any time.

---

#### 3.5.18.2 Actors

**Primary Actor**

- Customer

**Supporting Actors**

- Authentication Service
- Checkout Service
- Shopping Cart Service
- Order Management Service
- Payment Management Service
- Inventory Management Service
- Coupon Management Service
- Wallet Management Service
- Delivery Management Service
- Session Management Service
- Notification Service
- Audit Logging Service
- Database Management System

---

#### 3.5.18.3 Preconditions

Before cancelling the checkout, the following conditions shall be satisfied:

1. Customer shall be authenticated.
2. Customer account shall be active.
3. A valid checkout session shall exist.
4. Checkout session shall not have expired.
5. Checkout process shall be in progress.
6. No order shall have been successfully created.
7. Checkout session shall belong to the authenticated customer.
8. Checkout session shall not already be cancelled.
9. Checkout session shall not already be completed.
10. Shopping Cart Service shall be operational.
11. Checkout Service shall be operational.
12. Session Management Service shall be operational.
13. Payment Management Service shall be operational.
14. Inventory Management Service shall be operational.
15. Audit Logging Service shall be operational.
16. Database connectivity shall be available.
17. Customer shall have permission to cancel the checkout.
18. All dependent backend services shall be operational.

---

#### 3.5.18.4 Trigger

The use case is initiated when the customer clicks the **Cancel Checkout** button or chooses to exit the checkout process before successfully placing the order.

---

#### 3.5.18.5 Input Fields

| Field Name | Description | Mandatory | Validation |
|------------|-------------|-----------|------------|
| Checkout Session ID | Active checkout session identifier | Yes | Must be valid |
| Customer ID | Authenticated customer identifier | Yes | Must match logged-in customer |
| Cancellation Reason | Customer-selected or entered reason | No | Maximum configured length |
| Confirmation Flag | Customer confirmation to cancel checkout | Yes | Must be explicitly confirmed |
| Device Information | Device details for audit purposes | No | Valid format |
| Checkout Version | Current checkout version | Yes | Must match active checkout session |
| Cancellation Timestamp | Date and time of cancellation | Yes | System generated |
| Source Channel | Mobile App, Web, Tablet, etc. | Yes | Supported channel value |

#### 3.5.18.6 Business Rules

1. Only authenticated customers shall be allowed to cancel an active checkout session.
2. Checkout cancellation shall be permitted only before successful order creation.
3. Customers shall explicitly confirm checkout cancellation before processing.
4. A cancelled checkout session shall not be reused for future order placement.
5. The system shall terminate the active checkout session upon successful cancellation.
6. Any temporary checkout locks shall be released.
7. Any temporary inventory reservations shall be released.
8. Any temporary payment authorization shall be cancelled or voided, where applicable.
9. Any temporary wallet reservation shall be released, where applicable.
10. Any temporary coupon reservation shall be released, where applicable.
11. Any temporary delivery slot reservation shall be released, where applicable.
12. Shopping cart contents shall be preserved unless organizational policy specifies otherwise.
13. Customer-selected delivery address shall remain saved in the customer profile.
14. Customer preferences entered during checkout may be retained according to organizational policy.
15. No order record shall be created after successful checkout cancellation.
16. No payment shall be captured.
17. No invoice shall be generated.
18. No delivery request shall be created.
19. No restaurant notification shall be sent.
20. Cancellation timestamp shall be generated by the system.
21. Cancellation reason shall be recorded when provided.
22. Cancellation event shall be associated with the authenticated customer.
23. Cancellation event shall be associated with the active checkout session.
24. Successful cancellation shall be recorded in audit logs.
25. Failed cancellation attempts shall also be recorded.
26. Duplicate cancellation requests shall be processed idempotently.
27. Expired checkout sessions shall not be cancelled.
28. Completed checkout sessions shall not be cancelled.
29. All dependent services shall complete rollback operations successfully before cancellation is finalized.
30. Cancellation shall maintain transactional consistency across all services.
31. Sensitive customer information shall remain protected throughout the cancellation process.
32. Organizational checkout policies shall be configurable.
33. Cancellation analytics shall be available for reporting purposes.
34. Cancellation processing shall comply with organizational security and compliance requirements.
35. The system shall maintain complete data integrity throughout the cancellation workflow.

---

#### 3.5.18.7 Validations

1. Verify customer authentication.
2. Verify customer account status.
3. Verify checkout session validity.
4. Verify checkout session ownership.
5. Verify checkout session has not expired.
6. Verify checkout session has not already been cancelled.
7. Verify checkout session has not already been completed.
8. Verify no order has been successfully created.
9. Verify customer confirmation for cancellation.
10. Verify Shopping Cart Service availability.
11. Verify Checkout Service availability.
12. Verify Session Management Service availability.
13. Verify Payment Management Service availability.
14. Verify Inventory Management Service availability.
15. Verify Coupon Management Service availability, where applicable.
16. Verify Wallet Management Service availability, where applicable.
17. Verify Delivery Management Service availability, where applicable.
18. Verify Audit Logging Service availability.
19. Verify database connectivity.
20. Verify checkout version consistency.
21. Verify duplicate cancellation requests.
22. Verify rollback operation readiness.
23. Verify secure transmission of the cancellation request.
24. Verify cancellation timestamp generation.
25. Verify cancellation record persistence.
26. Verify audit event generation.
27. Verify all dependent rollback operations complete successfully.
28. Verify transactional consistency before completing the cancellation process.

#### 3.5.18.8 Main Flow

1. Customer reviews the active checkout page.
2. Customer decides not to continue with the checkout.
3. Customer clicks the **Cancel Checkout** button.
4. System displays a checkout cancellation confirmation dialog.
5. Customer confirms the cancellation request.
6. System receives the cancellation request.
7. System validates the customer's authentication.
8. System validates the active checkout session.
9. System verifies that no order has been successfully created.
10. System verifies that the checkout session is eligible for cancellation.
11. System locks the checkout session to prevent concurrent operations.
12. System initiates the checkout cancellation process.
13. System releases any temporary inventory reservations, where applicable.
14. System cancels or voids any temporary payment authorizations, where applicable.
15. System releases any temporary wallet reservations, where applicable.
16. System releases any temporary coupon reservations, where applicable.
17. System releases any temporary delivery slot reservations, where applicable.
18. System preserves the customer's shopping cart according to organizational policy.
19. System records the cancellation reason, where provided.
20. System generates the cancellation timestamp.
21. System updates the checkout session status as **Cancelled**.
22. System terminates the active checkout session.
23. System associates the cancellation event with the authenticated customer.
24. System records the cancellation event in the audit logs.
25. System performs final consistency verification across dependent services.
26. System commits the successful cancellation transaction.
27. System displays a checkout cancellation confirmation message.
28. System redirects the customer to the shopping cart or home page.
29. Customer may continue shopping or exit the application.
30. Checkout cancellation process completes successfully.

---

#### 3.5.18.9 Alternate Flows

**AF-1: Customer Aborts Cancellation**

1. Customer clicks **Cancel Checkout**.
2. System displays a confirmation dialog.
3. Customer selects **Continue Checkout** instead.
4. Cancellation process is aborted.
5. Checkout session remains active.

---

**AF-2: Checkout Session Expired**

1. Customer submits the cancellation request.
2. System detects that the checkout session has expired.
3. Cancellation request is rejected.
4. Customer is informed that the session has expired.
5. Customer starts a new checkout session if required.

---

**AF-3: Order Already Created**

1. Cancellation request is received.
2. System detects that an order has already been created.
3. Checkout cancellation is denied.
4. Customer is informed that checkout can no longer be cancelled.
5. Customer is redirected to the order management process.

---

**AF-4: Payment Authorization Release Failure**

1. System attempts to void the temporary payment authorization.
2. Payment provider does not respond successfully.
3. System initiates retry or reconciliation procedures.
4. Cancellation remains pending until organizational policies are satisfied.
5. Customer receives an appropriate notification.

---

**AF-5: Inventory Release Failure**

1. System attempts to release reserved inventory.
2. Inventory Management Service reports an error.
3. Rollback or retry procedures are initiated.
4. Cancellation processing pauses until consistency is restored.
5. Support logs are updated.

---

**AF-6: Duplicate Cancellation Request**

1. Multiple cancellation requests are received.
2. System detects duplicate requests.
3. Only the first request is processed.
4. Remaining requests receive the existing response.
5. Data consistency is maintained.

---

**AF-7: Checkout Session Already Cancelled**

1. Customer submits another cancellation request.
2. System identifies the checkout session as already cancelled.
3. Existing cancellation status is returned.
4. No additional processing occurs.
5. Customer receives the current checkout status.

---

**AF-8: Audit Logging Service Unavailable**

1. Checkout cancellation completes successfully.
2. Audit Logging Service is temporarily unavailable.
3. Audit event is queued according to retry policies.
4. Customer workflow continues without interruption.

---

**AF-9: Rollback Operation Failure**

1. One or more rollback operations fail.
2. System initiates recovery procedures.
3. Cancellation remains incomplete until consistency is restored.
4. Operational alerts are generated.
5. Customer receives a temporary status message.

---

**AF-10: Internal System Error**

1. An unexpected application error occurs.
2. System records the exception.
3. Checkout session remains recoverable where possible.
4. Customer receives a generic error message.
5. Customer may retry the cancellation process later.

#### 3.5.18.10 Postconditions

Upon successful completion of the Cancel Checkout process, the following postconditions shall be satisfied:

1. The checkout session shall be successfully cancelled.
2. The checkout session status shall be updated to **Cancelled**.
3. The checkout session shall be permanently closed.
4. Any temporary checkout locks shall be released.
5. Any temporary inventory reservations shall be released.
6. Any temporary payment authorizations shall be cancelled or voided, where applicable.
7. Any temporary wallet reservations shall be released, where applicable.
8. Any temporary coupon reservations shall be released, where applicable.
9. Any temporary delivery slot reservations shall be released, where applicable.
10. Shopping cart contents shall be preserved according to organizational policy.
11. No order record shall exist for the cancelled checkout.
12. No payment shall be captured.
13. No invoice shall be generated.
14. Cancellation event shall be associated with the authenticated customer.
15. Cancellation event shall be associated with the checkout session.
16. Cancellation timestamp shall be permanently recorded.
17. Cancellation details shall be stored for operational reporting.
18. Successful cancellation shall be recorded in the audit logs.
19. Transactional consistency shall be maintained across all dependent services.
20. Customer shall be allowed to start a new checkout session at any time.

---

#### 3.5.18.11 Success Response

Upon successful checkout cancellation, the system shall:

1. Display a confirmation message indicating that the checkout has been successfully cancelled.
2. Display the cancellation timestamp.
3. Display the cancellation reference, where applicable.
4. Confirm that no order has been placed.
5. Confirm that no payment has been captured.
6. Confirm that temporary reservations have been released, where applicable.
7. Preserve the customer's shopping cart according to organizational policy.
8. Update the checkout session status to **Cancelled**.
9. Persist the cancellation record.
10. Record the successful cancellation event in the audit logs.
11. Maintain transactional consistency across all services.
12. Redirect the customer to the shopping cart, home page, or another configured destination.

---

#### 3.5.18.12 Failure Response

If the Cancel Checkout process cannot be completed, the system shall:

1. Reject the cancellation request.
2. Display an appropriate validation or system error message.
3. Inform the customer if the checkout session has expired.
4. Inform the customer if an order has already been created.
5. Inform the customer if rollback operations cannot be completed.
6. Inform the customer if dependent services are unavailable.
7. Preserve the checkout session whenever possible.
8. Prevent inconsistent checkout states.
9. Record failure details in application logs.
10. Maintain transactional consistency.
11. Allow the customer to retry after the identified issue has been resolved.
12. Escalate unrecoverable failures according to operational policies.

---

#### 3.5.18.13 Acceptance Criteria

The Cancel Checkout functionality shall be considered successfully implemented when all the following conditions are satisfied:

1. Only authenticated customers can cancel checkout.
2. Checkout session ownership is successfully verified.
3. Checkout can be cancelled only before order creation.
4. Customer confirmation is required before cancellation.
5. Checkout session is updated to **Cancelled**.
6. Temporary checkout locks are released.
7. Temporary inventory reservations are released.
8. Temporary payment authorizations are cancelled or voided where applicable.
9. Temporary wallet reservations are released where applicable.
10. Temporary coupon reservations are released where applicable.
11. Temporary delivery slot reservations are released where applicable.
12. Shopping cart is preserved according to organizational policy.
13. No order is created after cancellation.
14. No payment is captured.
15. Cancellation timestamp is generated.
16. Cancellation record is associated with the authenticated customer.
17. Duplicate cancellation requests are handled safely.
18. Successful cancellation events are recorded in audit logs.
19. Transactional consistency is maintained across all dependent services.
20. Customer can initiate a new checkout session after successful cancellation.

---

#### 3.5.18.14 Security Considerations

1. Only authenticated customers shall be permitted to cancel checkout.
2. Customer authorization shall be verified before processing cancellation requests.
3. Checkout Session IDs shall be validated before processing.
4. All communication shall occur over HTTPS with TLS encryption.
5. Cancellation requests shall be protected against replay attacks.
6. Sensitive checkout information shall be encrypted during transmission and storage.
7. Personally Identifiable Information (PII) shall be protected according to organizational privacy policies.
8. Role-based access control shall restrict cancellation operations to authorized users and backend services.
9. All requests shall be protected against SQL Injection, Cross-Site Scripting (XSS), Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF), parameter tampering, and other injection attacks.
10. Unauthorized cancellation attempts shall be blocked and recorded for security auditing.

---

#### 3.5.18.15 Audit Log Requirements

The system shall record the following audit events during the Cancel Checkout process:

1. Cancellation timestamp.
2. Customer ID.
3. Checkout Session ID.
4. Cancellation status.
5. Cancellation reason, where provided.
6. Customer IP Address.
7. Device Type.
8. Browser or Application Version.
9. Checkout Version.
10. Rollback operation status.
11. Released reservation details, where applicable.
12. Cancellation record identifier.
13. Validation failure reason, if applicable.
14. System processing duration.
15. Audit logs shall be immutable and retained according to the organization's retention policy.

---

#### 3.5.18.16 Assumptions

The Cancel Checkout functionality is implemented based on the following assumptions:

1. Customer authentication has already been completed.
2. A valid checkout session exists.
3. No order has been successfully created.
4. Checkout is currently in progress.
5. Shopping Cart Service is operational.
6. Checkout Service is operational.
7. Session Management Service is operational.
8. Payment Management Service is operational.
9. Audit Logging Service is operational.
10. All dependent backend services are operational.

---

#### 3.5.18.17 Dependencies

The Cancel Checkout functionality depends on the successful availability of the following components and services:

1. Authentication Service
2. Checkout Service
3. Shopping Cart Service
4. Order Management Service
5. Payment Management Service
6. Inventory Management Service
7. Coupon Management Service
8. Wallet Management Service
9. Delivery Management Service

### 3.5.19 Redirect to Payment

#### 3.5.19.1 Description

The Redirect to Payment use case enables the system to securely redirect the customer from the checkout process to the selected payment channel for payment authorization or payment completion. Depending on the selected payment method, the system may redirect the customer to an external payment gateway, banking application, UPI application, digital wallet, card processor, or an internal payment page.

Before initiating the redirection, the system shall perform final checkout validation, verify the payable amount, generate a unique payment transaction reference, and securely prepare the payment request. The payment request shall contain only the information required by the payment provider and shall comply with applicable security standards.

The system shall establish a secure payment session and transfer the customer to the payment interface using encrypted communication channels. Throughout the payment process, the checkout session shall remain active but locked to prevent modifications until the payment response is received or the payment session expires.

The redirection process shall not create an order, capture inventory permanently, or complete payment independently. Its purpose is solely to transfer the customer securely to the payment provider so that payment authorization or payment completion can occur.

---

#### 3.5.19.2 Actors

**Primary Actor**

- Customer

**Supporting Actors**

- Authentication Service
- Checkout Service
- Payment Management Service
- Payment Gateway
- Banking Network
- UPI Service Provider
- Digital Wallet Provider
- Fraud Detection Service
- Session Management Service
- Notification Service
- Audit Logging Service
- Database Management System

---

#### 3.5.19.3 Preconditions

Before redirecting the customer to payment, the following conditions shall be satisfied:

1. Customer shall be authenticated.
2. Customer account shall be active.
3. A valid checkout session shall exist.
4. Checkout session shall not have expired.
5. Checkout validation shall be completed successfully.
6. Order confirmation shall be completed.
7. Final payable amount shall be calculated.
8. Payment method shall be selected.
9. Selected payment method shall be active.
10. Mandatory legal consent shall be completed.
11. Checkout session shall not be cancelled.
12. Checkout session shall not already be redirected to payment.
13. Payment Management Service shall be operational.
14. Selected Payment Gateway shall be available.
15. Fraud Detection Service shall be operational.
16. Session Management Service shall be operational.
17. Audit Logging Service shall be operational.
18. All dependent backend services shall be operational.

---

#### 3.5.19.4 Trigger

The use case is initiated when the customer clicks the **Proceed to Payment** button after successfully confirming the order.

---

#### 3.5.19.5 Input Fields

| Field Name | Description | Mandatory | Validation |
|------------|-------------|-----------|------------|
| Checkout Session ID | Active checkout session identifier | Yes | Must be valid |
| Customer ID | Authenticated customer identifier | Yes | Must match logged-in customer |
| Payment Transaction Reference | Unique payment transaction identifier | Yes | System generated and unique |
| Payment Method | Selected payment method | Yes | Must be active and supported |
| Payment Gateway ID | Selected payment gateway | Yes | Must exist and be available |
| Final Payable Amount | Total amount to be paid | Yes | Must match checkout calculation |
| Currency Code | Payment currency | Yes | Supported ISO currency code |
| Success Callback URL | URL to receive successful payment response | Yes | Must be registered and valid |
| Failure Callback URL | URL to receive failed payment response | Yes | Must be registered and valid |
| Device Information | Device details for audit purposes | No | Valid format |
| Checkout Version | Current checkout version | Yes | Must match the active checkout session |

#### 3.5.19.6 Business Rules

1. Only authenticated customers shall be allowed to proceed to the payment stage.
2. Payment redirection shall be permitted only after successful order confirmation.
3. A valid checkout session shall exist before payment redirection.
4. The checkout session shall be locked before redirecting the customer.
5. The final payable amount shall remain unchanged during the redirection process.
6. A unique payment transaction reference shall be generated for every payment attempt.
7. Each payment attempt shall have a unique payment session identifier.
8. The selected payment method shall be validated before redirection.
9. The selected payment gateway shall be active and available.
10. Payment gateway selection shall comply with organizational routing policies.
11. Only supported currencies shall be accepted.
12. Payment request data shall be digitally signed where required.
13. Sensitive payment information shall never be stored in plain text.
14. The system shall transmit only the minimum required payment information.
15. Customer payment credentials shall never pass through unauthorized application components.
16. Payment communication shall occur only over secure encrypted channels.
17. Fraud detection validation shall be completed before redirection.
18. Duplicate payment redirection requests shall be handled idempotently.
19. Expired checkout sessions shall not be redirected to payment.
20. Cancelled checkout sessions shall not be redirected to payment.
21. Successfully completed payment sessions shall not be redirected again.
22. The payment session shall have a configurable expiration period.
23. Customer shall be redirected only to trusted and configured payment gateways.
24. Success and failure callback URLs shall be validated before use.
25. Payment redirection shall not create an order independently.
26. Payment redirection shall not permanently reserve inventory.
27. Payment redirection shall not capture payment by itself.
28. All payment attempts shall be recorded for audit purposes.
29. Failed payment redirection attempts shall also be recorded.
30. Organizational payment routing policies shall be configurable.
31. Payment processing shall comply with PCI DSS and applicable financial regulations.
32. Personally Identifiable Information (PII) shall be protected throughout the payment process.
33. Payment session information shall be securely stored.
34. Transactional consistency shall be maintained across all dependent services.
35. The system shall maintain complete integrity throughout the payment redirection process.

---

#### 3.5.19.7 Validations

1. Verify customer authentication.
2. Verify customer account status.
3. Verify checkout session validity.
4. Verify checkout session ownership.
5. Verify checkout session has not expired.
6. Verify checkout confirmation has been completed.
7. Verify payment method validity.
8. Verify payment gateway availability.
9. Verify payment transaction reference uniqueness.
10. Verify final payable amount consistency.
11. Verify supported currency.
12. Verify callback URL validity.
13. Verify payment session has not already been created.
14. Verify checkout session is not cancelled.
15. Verify checkout session is not completed.
16. Verify Fraud Detection Service availability.
17. Verify Payment Management Service availability.
18. Verify Payment Gateway availability.
19. Verify Session Management Service availability.
20. Verify Audit Logging Service availability.
21. Verify checkout version consistency.
22. Verify duplicate payment requests.
23. Verify secure payment request generation.
24. Verify payment request signature generation, where applicable.
25. Verify payment session creation.
26. Verify secure transmission of payment data.
27. Verify audit event generation.
28. Verify all dependent backend services are operational before initiating redirection.

#### 3.5.19.8 Main Flow

1. Customer reviews the final checkout summary.
2. Customer clicks the **Proceed to Payment** button.
3. System receives the payment redirection request.
4. System validates the customer's authentication.
5. System validates the active checkout session.
6. System verifies that the checkout session has not expired.
7. System verifies that order confirmation has been completed.
8. System validates the selected payment method.
9. System validates the selected payment gateway.
10. System verifies the final payable amount.
11. System verifies that pricing has not changed.
12. System performs fraud detection validation.
13. System generates a unique payment transaction reference.
14. System generates a unique payment session identifier.
15. System prepares the payment request payload.
16. System digitally signs the payment request where required.
17. System validates the success callback URL.
18. System validates the failure callback URL.
19. System securely stores the payment session details.
20. System locks the checkout session against further modifications.
21. System records the payment initiation event.
22. System records the payment initiation details in the audit logs.
23. System establishes a secure communication channel with the payment gateway.
24. System submits the payment request to the selected payment gateway.
25. Payment gateway validates the incoming request.
26. Payment gateway creates the customer payment session.
27. System receives the payment session confirmation.
28. System redirects the customer's browser or mobile application to the payment gateway.
29. Customer is presented with the payment interface.
30. Customer proceeds with payment authorization or payment completion.

---

#### 3.5.19.9 Alternate Flows

**AF-1: Customer Cancels Before Redirection**

1. Customer clicks **Proceed to Payment**.
2. Customer cancels before the redirect occurs.
3. System aborts the payment initiation.
4. Checkout session remains active.
5. Customer returns to the checkout page.

---

**AF-2: Checkout Session Expired**

1. Payment request is received.
2. System detects that the checkout session has expired.
3. Payment redirection is rejected.
4. Customer is informed that the session has expired.
5. Customer starts a new checkout session.

---

**AF-3: Payment Gateway Unavailable**

1. System attempts to connect to the payment gateway.
2. Payment gateway is unavailable.
3. Payment session is not created.
4. Customer receives an appropriate notification.
5. Customer may retry or choose another payment method.

---

**AF-4: Fraud Detection Failed**

1. Fraud Detection Service identifies a suspicious transaction.
2. Payment redirection is blocked.
3. Security event is recorded.
4. Customer receives an appropriate notification.
5. Organizational fraud handling procedures are initiated.

---

**AF-5: Payment Request Signature Validation Failed**

1. System generates the payment request.
2. Digital signature validation fails.
3. Payment request is rejected.
4. System records the failure.
5. Customer may retry later.

---

**AF-6: Duplicate Payment Request**

1. Multiple payment requests are received.
2. System detects duplicate requests.
3. Only the first request is processed.
4. Remaining requests receive the existing response.
5. Duplicate payment sessions are prevented.

---

**AF-7: Callback URL Validation Failed**

1. System validates callback URLs.
2. One or more callback URLs are invalid.
3. Payment request is rejected.
4. Operational logs are updated.
5. Customer receives a temporary error message.

---

**AF-8: Payment Session Creation Failed**

1. Payment gateway cannot create the payment session.
2. Gateway returns an error response.
3. System records the failure.
4. Checkout session remains active.
5. Customer may retry payment.

---

**AF-9: Audit Logging Service Unavailable**

1. Payment initiation succeeds.
2. Audit Logging Service is temporarily unavailable.
3. Audit event is queued according to retry policies.
4. Customer workflow continues without interruption.

---

**AF-10: Internal System Error**

1. An unexpected application error occurs.
2. System records the exception.
3. Payment redirection is aborted.
4. Checkout session remains recoverable.
5. Customer receives a generic error message.

#### 3.5.19.10 Postconditions

Upon successful completion of the Redirect to Payment process, the following postconditions shall be satisfied:

1. A secure payment session shall be successfully established.
2. A unique payment transaction reference shall be permanently recorded.
3. A unique payment session identifier shall be generated and stored.
4. The customer shall be successfully redirected to the selected payment gateway.
5. The checkout session shall remain active but locked until the payment response is received.
6. The payment initiation status shall be updated appropriately.
7. Payment request details shall be securely stored.
8. Callback URLs shall be associated with the payment session.
9. Payment gateway reference information shall be recorded, where applicable.
10. Payment initiation timestamp shall be permanently stored.
11. Payment initiation event shall be associated with the authenticated customer.
12. Payment initiation event shall be associated with the active checkout session.
13. Successful payment initiation shall be recorded in the audit logs.
14. Transactional consistency shall be maintained across all dependent services.
15. The system shall wait for the payment gateway callback or customer return response.

---

#### 3.5.19.11 Success Response

Upon successful payment redirection, the system shall:

1. Successfully establish the payment session.
2. Generate a unique payment transaction reference.
3. Generate a unique payment session identifier.
4. Securely store payment initiation details.
5. Lock the checkout session against further modifications.
6. Redirect the customer to the selected payment gateway.
7. Display the payment provider interface.
8. Record the payment initiation timestamp.
9. Record the successful payment initiation event in the audit logs.
10. Maintain checkout session consistency.
11. Wait for the payment gateway response.
12. Continue the payment workflow after receiving the callback response.

---

#### 3.5.19.12 Failure Response

If the Redirect to Payment process cannot be completed, the system shall:

1. Reject the payment redirection request.
2. Display an appropriate validation or system error message.
3. Inform the customer if the checkout session has expired.
4. Inform the customer if the payment gateway is unavailable.
5. Inform the customer if payment session creation fails.
6. Inform the customer if fraud validation fails.
7. Preserve the checkout session whenever possible.
8. Unlock the checkout session if payment initiation was not completed.
9. Record failure details in application logs.
10. Maintain transactional consistency.
11. Allow the customer to retry payment or select another payment method.
12. Escalate unrecoverable failures according to operational policies.

---

#### 3.5.19.13 Acceptance Criteria

The Redirect to Payment functionality shall be considered successfully implemented when all the following conditions are satisfied:

1. Only authenticated customers can initiate payment.
2. Checkout session ownership is successfully verified.
3. Checkout session validity is verified.
4. Order confirmation is completed before payment redirection.
5. Payment method is successfully validated.
6. Payment gateway availability is verified.
7. Final payable amount is successfully verified.
8. Unique payment transaction reference is generated.
9. Unique payment session identifier is generated.
10. Callback URLs are successfully validated.
11. Payment request is securely generated.
12. Payment request is digitally signed where applicable.
13. Checkout session is locked during payment processing.
14. Customer is successfully redirected to the selected payment gateway.
15. Payment initiation timestamp is generated.
16. Duplicate payment requests are handled safely.
17. Successful payment initiation events are recorded in audit logs.
18. Sensitive payment information is protected.
19. Transactional consistency is maintained.
20. System correctly waits for the payment gateway callback.

---

#### 3.5.19.14 Security Considerations

1. Only authenticated customers shall be permitted to initiate payment.
2. Customer authorization shall be verified before payment initiation.
3. Checkout Session IDs shall be validated before processing.
4. All communications shall occur over HTTPS with TLS encryption.
5. Payment request payloads shall be digitally signed where required.
6. Sensitive payment information shall never be logged in plain text.
7. Payment credentials shall never be stored within the application unless permitted by applicable compliance requirements.
8. Personally Identifiable Information (PII) shall be protected according to organizational privacy policies.
9. All requests shall be protected against SQL Injection, Cross-Site Scripting (XSS), Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF), replay attacks, parameter tampering, and other injection attacks.
10. Payment processing shall comply with PCI DSS, RBI guidelines, and other applicable financial regulations.

---

The system shall record the following audit events during the Redirect to Payment process:

2. Customer ID.
3. Checkout Session ID.
4. Payment Transaction Reference.
5. Payment Session Identifier.
6. Selected Payment Method.
7. Selected Payment Gateway.
8. Final Payable Amount.
9. Payment initiation status.

11. Device Type.

13. Checkout Version.

15. Audit logs shall be immutable and retained according to the organization's retention policy.

---

The Redirect to Payment functionality is implemented based on the following assumptions:

2. Checkout validation has been successfully completed.
3. Order confirmation has been completed.

### 3.5.20 Handle Checkout Failure

#### 3.5.20.1 Description

The Handle Checkout Failure use case defines how the system manages failures that occur during the checkout process before an order is successfully created. Checkout failures may result from business validation failures, payment processing failures, inventory unavailability, pricing changes, coupon validation failures, network interruptions, service outages, timeout events, fraud detection failures, or unexpected internal system errors.

Upon detecting a failure, the system shall immediately stop the current checkout transaction, identify the root cause, perform all necessary rollback operations, maintain transactional consistency, and prevent partial or inconsistent data from being committed. Depending on the type of failure, the system may release temporary inventory reservations, cancel payment authorizations, unlock the checkout session, restore wallet reservations, release delivery slots, and preserve the customer's shopping cart for future checkout attempts.

The system shall provide meaningful failure information to the customer without exposing sensitive internal implementation details. Whenever possible, the customer shall be allowed to retry the checkout process after resolving the identified issue. All checkout failures shall be recorded for operational monitoring, analytics, security investigation, troubleshooting, and compliance auditing.

Handling checkout failures shall not create an order, capture payment, generate invoices, or initiate delivery workflows unless those operations have already been successfully completed according to organizational policies.

---

#### 3.5.20.2 Actors

**Primary Actor**

- Customer

**Supporting Actors**

- Authentication Service
- Checkout Service
- Order Management Service
- Payment Management Service
- Payment Gateway
- Inventory Management Service
- Pricing Service
- Coupon Management Service
- Wallet Management Service
- Delivery Management Service
- Fraud Detection Service
- Notification Service
- Session Management Service
- Audit Logging Service
- Database Management System

---

#### 3.5.20.3 Preconditions

Before handling a checkout failure, the following conditions shall be satisfied:

1. Customer shall be authenticated.
2. Customer account shall be active.
3. A checkout session shall exist.
4. Checkout processing shall be in progress.
5. A checkout failure shall be detected.
6. Checkout session shall belong to the authenticated customer.
7. Failure detection mechanism shall be operational.
8. Session Management Service shall be operational.
9. Checkout Service shall be operational.
10. Payment Management Service shall be operational.
11. Inventory Management Service shall be operational.
12. Notification Service shall be operational.
13. Audit Logging Service shall be operational.
14. Database connectivity shall be available.
15. Rollback mechanisms shall be available.
16. Error logging infrastructure shall be operational.
17. Customer shall have permission to retry checkout where applicable.
18. All dependent backend services shall be operational.

---

#### 3.5.20.4 Trigger

The use case is initiated automatically when the system detects a failure during any stage of the checkout process before successful order creation.

---

#### 3.5.20.5 Input Fields

| Field Name | Description | Mandatory | Validation |
|------------|-------------|-----------|------------|
| Checkout Session ID | Active checkout session identifier | Yes | Must be valid |
| Customer ID | Authenticated customer identifier | Yes | Must match logged-in customer |
| Failure Code | System-generated failure code | Yes | Must be recognized |
| Failure Category | Business, Payment, Inventory, System, Network, etc. | Yes | Supported value |
| Failure Message | Internal failure description | Yes | System generated |
| Payment Transaction Reference | Payment reference, if applicable | Conditional | Must exist for payment failures |
| Rollback Status | Rollback execution status | Yes | System generated |
| Device Information | Device details for audit purposes | No | Valid format |
| Checkout Version | Current checkout version | Yes | Must match active checkout session |
| Failure Timestamp | Date and time of failure | Yes | System generated |

#### 3.5.20.6 Business Rules

1. Checkout failures shall be detected immediately upon occurrence.
2. Only authenticated customers shall be allowed to resume or retry a failed checkout.
3. The system shall classify failures into predefined failure categories.
4. Every failure shall be assigned a unique failure code.
5. Checkout processing shall stop immediately after a failure is detected.
6. No order shall be created if checkout fails before order creation.
7. No payment shall be captured for unsuccessful checkout transactions.
8. Any temporary payment authorization shall be cancelled or voided, where applicable.
9. Any temporary inventory reservation shall be released.
10. Any temporary wallet reservation shall be released, where applicable.
11. Any temporary coupon reservation shall be released, where applicable.
12. Any temporary delivery slot reservation shall be released, where applicable.
13. The checkout session shall be unlocked after rollback completion.
14. Shopping cart contents shall be preserved according to organizational policy.
15. Customer delivery address selections may be retained according to organizational policy.
16. Customer delivery instructions may be retained where applicable.
17. Rollback operations shall be completed before marking the checkout as failed.
18. Partial transactions shall not remain committed.
19. Transactional consistency shall be maintained across all dependent services.
20. Customer shall receive a user-friendly failure message.
21. Internal system errors shall never be exposed to customers.
22. Retry eligibility shall depend on the failure category.
23. Duplicate failure processing requests shall be handled idempotently.
24. Failure analytics shall be maintained for operational reporting.
25. Failed checkout events shall be recorded in audit logs.
26. Security-related failures shall trigger additional monitoring where applicable.
27. Fraud-related failures shall follow organizational fraud handling procedures.
28. All rollback operations shall be logged.
29. Failure notifications shall be generated where configured.
30. Checkout failure handling shall comply with organizational security policies.
31. Checkout failure handling shall comply with applicable regulatory requirements.
32. Personally Identifiable Information (PII) shall remain protected during failure processing.
33. Failure recovery policies shall be configurable.
34. Operational alerts shall be generated for critical failures.
35. The system shall maintain complete data integrity throughout the checkout failure handling process.

---

#### 3.5.20.7 Validations

1. Verify customer authentication.
2. Verify customer account status.
3. Verify checkout session validity.
4. Verify checkout session ownership.
5. Verify failure detection event.
6. Verify failure category.
7. Verify failure code validity.
8. Verify rollback eligibility.
9. Verify no successful order has been created.
10. Verify payment transaction status, where applicable.
11. Verify inventory reservation status.
12. Verify coupon reservation status, where applicable.
13. Verify wallet reservation status, where applicable.
14. Verify delivery slot reservation status, where applicable.
15. Verify Checkout Service availability.
16. Verify Payment Management Service availability.
17. Verify Inventory Management Service availability.
18. Verify Session Management Service availability.
19. Verify Notification Service availability.
20. Verify Audit Logging Service availability.
21. Verify database connectivity.
22. Verify rollback execution status.
23. Verify checkout version consistency.
24. Verify duplicate failure processing requests.
25. Verify secure transmission of failure information.
26. Verify failure record persistence.
27. Verify audit event generation.
28. Verify successful completion of all rollback operations before finalizing the checkout failure.

#### 3.5.20.8 Main Flow

1. System detects a failure during the checkout process.
2. System immediately pauses further checkout processing.
3. System identifies the failure category.
4. System generates a unique failure code.
5. System records the failure timestamp.
6. System validates the active checkout session.
7. System verifies that no successful order has been created.
8. System determines the rollback actions required.
9. System locks the checkout session to prevent concurrent operations.
10. System cancels any pending payment authorization, where applicable.
11. System releases temporary inventory reservations.
12. System releases temporary wallet reservations, where applicable.
13. System releases temporary coupon reservations, where applicable.
14. System releases temporary delivery slot reservations, where applicable.
15. System rolls back incomplete checkout transactions.
16. System restores the shopping cart according to organizational policy.
17. System restores checkout data eligible for recovery.
18. System unlocks the checkout session after rollback completion.
19. System updates the checkout status as **Failed**.
20. System stores the failure details.
21. System associates the failure event with the authenticated customer.
22. System records the failure event in the audit logs.
23. System generates operational monitoring events.
24. System determines whether the checkout is eligible for retry.
25. System prepares an appropriate customer-facing failure message.
26. System displays the failure notification.
27. System displays retry options where applicable.
28. Customer reviews the failure information.
29. Customer retries the checkout or exits the checkout process.
30. Checkout failure handling process completes successfully.

---

#### 3.5.20.9 Alternate Flows

**AF-1: Payment Authorization Failure**

1. Payment gateway rejects the authorization request.
2. System identifies the payment failure.
3. Temporary payment authorization is cancelled, if created.
4. Customer is informed about the payment failure.
5. Customer selects another payment method or retries.

---

**AF-2: Inventory Unavailable**

1. Inventory validation fails.
2. System identifies unavailable menu items.
3. Reserved inventory is released.
4. Customer is prompted to modify the shopping cart.
5. Checkout resumes after cart updates.

---

**AF-3: Pricing Changed During Checkout**

1. System detects updated pricing.
2. Checkout totals are recalculated.
3. Customer is informed about the revised price.
4. Previous checkout transaction is terminated.
5. Customer starts checkout again using updated pricing.

---

**AF-4: Coupon Validation Failure**

1. Coupon validation fails.
2. System removes the invalid coupon.
3. Checkout totals are recalculated.
4. Customer is informed about the invalid coupon.
5. Customer continues without the coupon or applies another one.

---

**AF-5: Wallet Reservation Failure**

1. Wallet reservation cannot be completed.
2. System releases any partial wallet reservation.
3. Customer is notified of the issue.
4. Checkout remains recoverable.
5. Customer retries or selects another payment method.

---

**AF-6: Network Timeout**

1. Network timeout occurs during checkout.
2. System safely terminates the incomplete transaction.
3. Rollback operations are executed.
4. Checkout session is preserved where possible.
5. Customer retries after connectivity is restored.

---

**AF-7: Fraud Detection Failure**

1. Fraud Detection Service flags the transaction.
2. Checkout processing is immediately stopped.
3. Security event is recorded.
4. Customer receives an appropriate notification.
5. Organizational fraud investigation procedures are initiated.

---

**AF-8: Rollback Failure**

1. One or more rollback operations fail.
2. System initiates recovery procedures.
3. Operational alerts are generated.
4. Checkout remains in a recoverable state where possible.
5. Support personnel investigate the issue.

---

**AF-9: Audit Logging Service Unavailable**

1. Checkout failure handling completes successfully.
2. Audit Logging Service is temporarily unavailable.
3. Audit event is queued according to retry policies.
4. Customer workflow continues without interruption.

---

**AF-10: Internal System Error**

1. An unexpected application error occurs.
2. System records the exception.
3. Remaining rollback operations are attempted.
4. Customer receives a generic failure message.
5. Customer may retry the checkout later.

#### 3.5.20.10 Postconditions

Upon successful completion of the Handle Checkout Failure process, the following postconditions shall be satisfied:

1. The checkout transaction shall be marked as **Failed**.
2. Checkout processing shall be terminated safely.
3. No order shall be created for the failed checkout.
4. No payment shall be captured for the failed transaction.
5. Any temporary payment authorization shall be cancelled or voided, where applicable.
6. Any temporary inventory reservations shall be released.
7. Any temporary wallet reservations shall be released, where applicable.
8. Any temporary coupon reservations shall be released, where applicable.
9. Any temporary delivery slot reservations shall be released, where applicable.
10. Shopping cart contents shall be restored according to organizational policy.
11. Recoverable checkout information shall be preserved where applicable.
12. Checkout session shall be unlocked after rollback completion.
13. Failure details shall be permanently stored.
14. Failure event shall be associated with the authenticated customer.
15. Failure timestamp shall be permanently recorded.
16. Rollback status shall be recorded.
17. Operational monitoring events shall be generated.
18. Successful failure handling shall be recorded in the audit logs.
19. Transactional consistency shall be maintained across all dependent services.
20. Customer shall be allowed to retry checkout where organizational policies permit.

---

#### 3.5.20.11 Success Response

Upon successful handling of the checkout failure, the system shall:

1. Display an appropriate checkout failure message.
2. Display the failure reference number, where applicable.
3. Display retry options where permitted.
4. Preserve the customer's shopping cart.
5. Confirm that no order has been placed.
6. Confirm that no payment has been captured.
7. Confirm successful rollback of temporary resources where applicable.
8. Update the checkout status to **Failed**.
9. Persist the failure record.
10. Record the successful failure handling event in the audit logs.
11. Maintain transactional consistency.
12. Allow the customer to retry checkout or continue shopping.

---

#### 3.5.20.12 Failure Response

If the checkout failure cannot be handled successfully, the system shall:

1. Display a generic system error message.
2. Inform the customer that checkout could not be completed.
3. Prevent further checkout processing.
4. Attempt recovery procedures where supported.
5. Preserve recoverable checkout information whenever possible.
6. Generate operational alerts.
7. Record failure handling details in application logs.
8. Record incomplete rollback information.
9. Maintain the safest possible transactional state.
10. Prevent duplicate processing.
11. Allow the customer to retry later where appropriate.
12. Escalate unrecoverable failures according to organizational support procedures.

---

#### 3.5.20.13 Acceptance Criteria

The Handle Checkout Failure functionality shall be considered successfully implemented when all the following conditions are satisfied:

1. Checkout failures are detected automatically.
2. Failure categories are correctly identified.
3. Unique failure codes are generated.
4. Checkout processing stops immediately after failure detection.
5. No order is created after a checkout failure.
6. No payment is captured for failed transactions.
7. Temporary payment authorizations are cancelled where applicable.
8. Temporary inventory reservations are released.
9. Temporary wallet reservations are released where applicable.
10. Temporary coupon reservations are released where applicable.
11. Temporary delivery slot reservations are released where applicable.
12. Shopping cart is restored according to organizational policy.
13. Checkout session is unlocked after rollback.
14. Failure timestamp is generated.
15. Failure event is associated with the authenticated customer.
16. Rollback operations complete successfully.
17. Duplicate failure handling requests are processed safely.
18. Successful failure handling events are recorded in audit logs.
19. Transactional consistency is maintained across all dependent services.
20. Customer can retry checkout when permitted by organizational policies.

---

#### 3.5.20.14 Security Considerations

1. Only authenticated customers shall be permitted to resume failed checkout sessions.
2. Customer authorization shall be verified before allowing retry operations.
3. Checkout Session IDs shall be validated before processing.
4. All communications shall occur over HTTPS with TLS encryption.
5. Sensitive checkout and payment information shall never be exposed in customer-facing error messages.
6. Personally Identifiable Information (PII) shall be protected according to organizational privacy policies.
7. Failure records shall be securely stored and accessible only to authorized personnel.
8. Role-based access control shall restrict administrative access to failure management functions.
9. All requests shall be protected against SQL Injection, Cross-Site Scripting (XSS), Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF), replay attacks, parameter tampering, and other injection attacks.
10. Security-related failures shall trigger monitoring and alerting mechanisms according to organizational security policies.

---

The Handle Checkout Failure functionality is implemented based on the following assumptions:

5. Checkout Service is operational.
6. Payment Management Service is operational.
7. Inventory Management Service is operational.

### 3.5.21 Save Checkout Session

#### 3.5.21.1 Description

The Save Checkout Session use case enables the customer to temporarily save the current checkout progress and resume it later without losing previously entered checkout information. The system shall securely persist the checkout session, including selected cart items, delivery address, delivery preferences, payment method selection, applied coupons, wallet usage, delivery instructions, and other eligible checkout data according to organizational policies.

The saved checkout session shall have a configurable validity period. During this period, the customer may resume the checkout from the last saved state after successful authentication. Before restoring a saved session, the system shall revalidate dynamic information such as menu availability, pricing, delivery serviceability, promotional offers, estimated delivery time, and restaurant operational status to ensure data consistency.

Saving a checkout session shall not create an order, reserve inventory permanently, authorize payment, or initiate any delivery workflow. The feature is intended solely to improve customer convenience by allowing interrupted checkout sessions to be resumed later.

All save operations shall be securely recorded for auditing, analytics, customer support, and operational monitoring.

---

#### 3.5.21.2 Actors

**Primary Actor**

- Customer

**Supporting Actors**

- Authentication Service
- Checkout Service
- Shopping Cart Service
- Session Management Service
- Customer Management Service
- Pricing Service
- Coupon Management Service
- Wallet Management Service
- Notification Service
- Audit Logging Service
- Database Management System

---

#### 3.5.21.3 Preconditions

Before saving a checkout session, the following conditions shall be satisfied:

1. Customer shall be authenticated.
2. Customer account shall be active.
3. A valid checkout session shall exist.
4. Checkout session shall not have expired.
5. Checkout session shall belong to the authenticated customer.
6. Shopping cart shall contain at least one item.
7. Checkout process shall be in progress.
8. Checkout session shall not already be completed.
9. Checkout session shall not already be cancelled.
10. Session Management Service shall be operational.
11. Checkout Service shall be operational.
12. Shopping Cart Service shall be operational.
13. Customer Management Service shall be operational.
14. Database connectivity shall be available.
15. Audit Logging Service shall be operational.
16. Customer shall have permission to save the checkout session.
17. Save Checkout feature shall be enabled by organizational configuration.
18. All dependent backend services shall be operational.

---

#### 3.5.21.4 Trigger

The use case is initiated when the customer clicks the **Save Checkout** button or when the system automatically saves the checkout session according to configured auto-save policies.

---

#### 3.5.21.5 Input Fields

| Field Name | Description | Mandatory | Validation |
|------------|-------------|-----------|------------|
| Checkout Session ID | Active checkout session identifier | Yes | Must be valid |
| Customer ID | Authenticated customer identifier | Yes | Must match logged-in customer |
| Shopping Cart ID | Customer shopping cart identifier | Yes | Must exist |
| Save Type | Manual Save or Auto Save | Yes | Supported value |
| Delivery Address ID | Selected delivery address | Conditional | Must exist if selected |
| Payment Method | Selected payment method | Conditional | Must be valid if selected |
| Applied Coupon Code | Applied promotional coupon | Conditional | Must be valid if applied |
| Wallet Usage Amount | Wallet amount selected | Conditional | Must be valid if used |
| Delivery Instructions | Customer delivery notes | Conditional | Maximum configured length |
| Checkout Version | Current checkout version | Yes | Must match active checkout session |
| Save Timestamp | Date and time of save | Yes | System generated |

#### 3.5.21.6 Business Rules

1. Only authenticated customers shall be allowed to save a checkout session.
2. A checkout session shall be saved only if it is in an active state.
3. Completed checkout sessions shall not be eligible for saving.
4. Cancelled checkout sessions shall not be eligible for saving.
5. Expired checkout sessions shall not be eligible for saving.
6. The checkout session shall belong to the authenticated customer.
7. The system shall save only the latest version of the checkout session.
8. Saving a checkout session shall not create an order.
9. Saving a checkout session shall not authorize or capture payment.
10. Saving a checkout session shall not permanently reserve inventory.
11. Shopping cart contents shall be preserved during the save operation.
12. Selected delivery address shall be saved where applicable.
13. Selected delivery type shall be saved where applicable.
14. Selected payment method shall be saved where applicable.
15. Applied coupon information shall be saved where applicable.
16. Wallet usage preferences shall be saved where applicable.
17. Delivery instructions shall be saved where applicable.
18. Save timestamp shall be generated by the system.
19. Saved sessions shall have a configurable expiration period.
20. Expired saved sessions shall be automatically invalidated.
21. The latest saved session shall overwrite previous saved versions of the same checkout session.
22. Auto-save frequency shall be configurable by organizational policy.
23. Manual save requests shall take precedence over pending auto-save operations.
24. Duplicate save requests shall be handled idempotently.
25. Sensitive payment credentials shall never be stored in the saved session.
26. Personally Identifiable Information (PII) shall be protected during storage.
27. Saved checkout data shall be encrypted at rest.
28. Successful save operations shall be recorded in audit logs.
29. Failed save operations shall also be recorded.
30. Save operations shall maintain transactional consistency.
31. Customers shall be allowed to resume eligible saved sessions before expiration.
32. Dynamic checkout information shall be revalidated when the session is resumed.
33. Organizational retention policies shall govern saved session lifecycle.
34. Save functionality shall comply with organizational security and compliance requirements.
35. The system shall maintain complete data integrity throughout the save process.

---

#### 3.5.21.7 Validations

1. Verify customer authentication.
2. Verify customer account status.
3. Verify checkout session validity.
4. Verify checkout session ownership.
5. Verify checkout session has not expired.
6. Verify checkout session is active.
7. Verify checkout session has not already been completed.
8. Verify checkout session has not been cancelled.
9. Verify shopping cart contains at least one item.
10. Verify Shopping Cart Service availability.
11. Verify Checkout Service availability.
12. Verify Session Management Service availability.
13. Verify Customer Management Service availability.
14. Verify database connectivity.
15. Verify Audit Logging Service availability.
16. Verify save feature configuration.
17. Verify checkout version consistency.
18. Verify save request uniqueness.
19. Verify selected delivery address validity, where applicable.
20. Verify selected payment method validity, where applicable.
21. Verify applied coupon validity, where applicable.
22. Verify wallet usage information, where applicable.
23. Verify delivery instructions format and length.
24. Verify secure persistence of saved session data.
25. Verify save timestamp generation.
26. Verify successful session persistence.
27. Verify audit event generation.
28. Verify transactional consistency before completing the save operation.

#### 3.5.21.8 Main Flow

1. Customer is in an active checkout session.
2. Customer clicks the **Save Checkout** button or the system triggers an auto-save operation.
3. System receives the save checkout request.
4. System validates the customer's authentication.
5. System validates the active checkout session.
6. System verifies that the checkout session belongs to the authenticated customer.
7. System verifies that the checkout session is eligible for saving.
8. System verifies that the shopping cart contains at least one item.
9. System retrieves the latest checkout information.
10. System retrieves the latest shopping cart details.
11. System retrieves the selected delivery address, where applicable.
12. System retrieves the selected delivery type, where applicable.
13. System retrieves the selected payment method, where applicable.
14. System retrieves the applied coupon details, where applicable.
15. System retrieves the wallet usage information, where applicable.
16. System retrieves the delivery instructions, where applicable.
17. System generates the save timestamp.
18. System determines the configured expiration time for the saved session.
19. System encrypts the eligible checkout session data.
20. System persists the saved checkout session.
21. System updates the saved session metadata.
22. System associates the saved session with the authenticated customer.
23. System records the save operation in the audit logs.
24. System verifies successful persistence of the saved session.
25. System updates the checkout session status as **Saved**.
26. System generates a save confirmation response.
27. System displays a confirmation message to the customer.
28. Customer continues shopping or exits the application.
29. Saved checkout session remains available until expiration or successful completion.
30. Save Checkout process completes successfully.

---

#### 3.5.21.9 Alternate Flows

**AF-1: Auto Save Triggered**

1. Auto-save interval is reached.
2. System automatically initiates the save operation.
3. Checkout session is validated.
4. Session is saved successfully.
5. Customer continues checkout without interruption.

---

**AF-2: Checkout Session Expired**

1. Save request is received.
2. System detects that the checkout session has expired.
3. Save request is rejected.
4. Customer is informed that the session has expired.
5. Customer starts a new checkout session.

---

**AF-3: Empty Shopping Cart**

1. Save request is received.
2. System detects that the shopping cart is empty.
3. Save operation is cancelled.
4. Customer receives an appropriate notification.
5. Customer continues shopping.

---

**AF-4: Database Storage Failure**

1. System attempts to persist the saved checkout session.
2. Database operation fails.
3. Save operation is rolled back.
4. Failure is recorded.
5. Customer is informed that the session could not be saved.

---

**AF-5: Encryption Failure**

1. System encrypts the checkout session data.
2. Encryption process fails.
3. Save operation is aborted.
4. Security event is logged.
5. Customer receives a generic error message.

---

**AF-6: Duplicate Save Request**

1. Multiple save requests are received.
2. System detects duplicate requests.
3. Only one save operation is processed.
4. Remaining requests receive the existing response.
5. Duplicate saved sessions are prevented.

---

**AF-7: Save Feature Disabled**

1. Save request is received.
2. System detects that the Save Checkout feature is disabled.
3. Save request is rejected.
4. Customer receives an appropriate notification.
5. Customer continues checkout normally.

---

**AF-8: Audit Logging Service Unavailable**

1. Save operation completes successfully.
2. Audit Logging Service is temporarily unavailable.
3. Audit event is queued according to retry policies.
4. Customer workflow continues without interruption.

---

**AF-9: Session Already Saved**

1. Save request is received.
2. System detects an existing saved version.
3. Existing saved session is updated.
4. Latest version replaces the previous version.
5. Customer receives a successful save confirmation.

---

**AF-10: Internal System Error**

1. An unexpected application error occurs.
2. System records the exception.
3. Save operation is aborted.
4. Checkout session remains active.
5. Customer may retry the save operation later.

#### 3.5.21.10 Postconditions

Upon successful completion of the Save Checkout Session process, the following postconditions shall be satisfied:

1. The checkout session shall be successfully saved.
2. The latest checkout state shall be securely persisted.
3. The saved checkout session shall be associated with the authenticated customer.
4. The shopping cart shall remain unchanged.
5. Selected delivery address shall be preserved, where applicable.
6. Selected delivery type shall be preserved, where applicable.
7. Selected payment method shall be preserved, where applicable.
8. Applied coupon information shall be preserved, where applicable.
9. Wallet usage information shall be preserved, where applicable.
10. Delivery instructions shall be preserved, where applicable.
11. Save timestamp shall be permanently recorded.
12. Saved session expiration timestamp shall be recorded.
13. Checkout session status shall be updated as **Saved**.
14. Saved session metadata shall be stored.
15. Successful save operation shall be recorded in the audit logs.
16. Checkout session shall remain eligible for restoration until expiration.
17. Transactional consistency shall be maintained across all dependent services.
18. Customer shall be able to resume the saved checkout session before it expires.

---

#### 3.5.21.11 Success Response

Upon successful completion of the Save Checkout Session process, the system shall:

1. Display a confirmation message indicating that the checkout session has been successfully saved.
2. Display the save timestamp.
3. Display the saved session expiration time, where applicable.
4. Confirm that checkout progress has been preserved.
5. Preserve the customer's shopping cart.
6. Persist the encrypted checkout session data.
7. Associate the saved session with the authenticated customer.
8. Update the checkout session status to **Saved**.
9. Record the successful save event in the audit logs.
10. Maintain transactional consistency.
11. Allow the customer to safely exit the application.
12. Allow the customer to resume the checkout session before expiration.

---

#### 3.5.21.12 Failure Response

If the Save Checkout Session process cannot be completed, the system shall:

1. Reject the save request.
2. Display an appropriate validation or system error message.
3. Inform the customer if the checkout session has expired.
4. Inform the customer if the save feature is unavailable.
5. Inform the customer if data persistence fails.
6. Inform the customer if encryption fails.
7. Preserve the active checkout session whenever possible.
8. Prevent incomplete session storage.
9. Record failure details in application logs.
10. Maintain transactional consistency.
11. Allow the customer to retry the save operation.
12. Escalate unrecoverable failures according to organizational operational policies.

---

#### 3.5.21.13 Acceptance Criteria

The Save Checkout Session functionality shall be considered successfully implemented when all the following conditions are satisfied:

1. Only authenticated customers can save checkout sessions.
2. Checkout session ownership is successfully verified.
3. Active checkout sessions are successfully saved.
4. Completed checkout sessions cannot be saved.
5. Cancelled checkout sessions cannot be saved.
6. Shopping cart contents are preserved.
7. Selected delivery address is preserved where applicable.
8. Selected payment method is preserved where applicable.
9. Applied coupon information is preserved where applicable.
10. Wallet usage information is preserved where applicable.
11. Delivery instructions are preserved where applicable.
12. Save timestamp is generated.
13. Session expiration timestamp is generated.
14. Saved checkout data is encrypted.
15. Duplicate save requests are handled safely.
16. Successful save events are recorded in audit logs.
17. Saved checkout sessions remain recoverable before expiration.
18. Dynamic checkout information is revalidated upon restoration.
19. Transactional consistency is maintained.
20. Customers can successfully resume saved checkout sessions.

---

#### 3.5.21.14 Security Considerations

1. Only authenticated customers shall be permitted to save checkout sessions.
2. Customer authorization shall be verified before saving checkout data.
3. Checkout Session IDs shall be validated before processing.
4. All communications shall occur over HTTPS with TLS encryption.
5. Saved checkout data shall be encrypted at rest using approved encryption standards.
6. Sensitive payment credentials shall never be stored within the saved session.
7. Personally Identifiable Information (PII) shall be protected according to organizational privacy policies.
8. Role-based access control shall restrict access to saved checkout sessions.
9. All requests shall be protected against SQL Injection, Cross-Site Scripting (XSS), Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF), replay attacks, parameter tampering, and other injection attacks.
10. Unauthorized save or restore attempts shall be blocked and recorded for security auditing.

---

#### 3.5.21.15 Audit Log Requirements

The system shall record the following audit events during the Save Checkout Session process:

1. Save timestamp.
2. Customer ID.
3. Checkout Session ID.
4. Shopping Cart ID.
5. Save type (Manual or Auto Save).
6. Saved session status.
7. Session expiration timestamp.
8. Customer IP Address.
9. Device Type.
10. Browser or Application Version.
11. Checkout Version.
12. Saved session identifier.
13. Validation failure reason, where applicable.
14. Save processing duration.
15. Audit logs shall be immutable and retained according to the organization's retention policy.

---

#### 3.5.21.16 Assumptions

The Save Checkout Session functionality is implemented based on the following assumptions:

1. Customer authentication has already been completed.
2. A valid checkout session exists.
3. Shopping cart contains at least one item.
4. Save Checkout feature is enabled.
5. Checkout Service is operational.
6. Session Management Service is operational.
7. Shopping Cart Service is operational.
