### 3.5.22 Resume Interrupted Checkout

#### 3.5.22.1 Description

The Resume Interrupted Checkout use case enables a customer to continue a previously interrupted or saved checkout session without restarting the entire checkout process. The system shall securely retrieve the customer's most recent eligible checkout session, restore the saved checkout information, and revalidate all dynamic business data before allowing the customer to continue.

During the restoration process, the system shall verify menu item availability, restaurant operational status, pricing, taxes, discounts, coupon validity, wallet balance, delivery address serviceability, delivery availability, estimated delivery time, and payment method eligibility. Any information that has changed since the session was saved shall be updated before the checkout is resumed.

If any saved checkout information is no longer valid, the system shall notify the customer, automatically refresh the affected information where possible, and require the customer to review the updated checkout summary before continuing.

Resuming an interrupted checkout shall not create an order, authorize payment, reserve inventory permanently, or initiate delivery workflows. The feature is intended to improve customer convenience while maintaining business consistency, security, and data integrity.

All resume operations shall be securely recorded for auditing, analytics, troubleshooting, fraud monitoring, and operational reporting.

---

#### 3.5.22.2 Actors

**Primary Actor**

- Customer

**Supporting Actors**

- Authentication Service
- Checkout Service
- Session Management Service
- Shopping Cart Service
- Restaurant Management Service
- Menu Management Service
- Pricing Service
- Coupon Management Service
- Wallet Management Service
- Delivery Management Service
- Notification Service
- Audit Logging Service
- Database Management System

---

#### 3.5.22.3 Preconditions

Before resuming an interrupted checkout, the following conditions shall be satisfied:

1. Customer shall be authenticated.
2. Customer account shall be active.
3. A previously saved checkout session shall exist.
4. The saved checkout session shall belong to the authenticated customer.
5. The saved checkout session shall not have expired.
6. The saved checkout session shall not already be completed.
7. The saved checkout session shall not have been cancelled.
8. Checkout Service shall be operational.
9. Session Management Service shall be operational.
10. Shopping Cart Service shall be operational.
11. Pricing Service shall be operational.
12. Restaurant Management Service shall be operational.
13. Menu Management Service shall be operational.
14. Delivery Management Service shall be operational.
15. Coupon Management Service shall be operational.
16. Audit Logging Service shall be operational.
17. Database connectivity shall be available.
18. All dependent backend services shall be operational.

---

#### 3.5.22.4 Trigger

The use case is initiated when the customer selects a previously saved or interrupted checkout session by clicking the **Resume Checkout** button.

---

#### 3.5.22.5 Input Fields

| Field Name | Description | Mandatory | Validation |
|------------|-------------|-----------|------------|
| Saved Checkout Session ID | Identifier of the saved checkout session | Yes | Must exist and belong to the authenticated customer |
| Customer ID | Authenticated customer identifier | Yes | Must match logged-in customer |
| Shopping Cart ID | Associated shopping cart identifier | Yes | Must exist |
| Resume Request ID | Unique resume operation identifier | Yes | System generated |
| Checkout Version | Saved checkout version | Yes | Must be valid |
| Device Information | Device details for audit purposes | No | Valid format |
| Resume Timestamp | Date and time of resume request | Yes | System generated |
| Source Channel | Web, Mobile App, Tablet, etc. | Yes | Supported channel value |
| Session Token | Authenticated session token | Yes | Must be valid |
| Preferred Language | Customer language preference | No | Supported language code |

#### 3.5.22.6 Business Rules

1. Only authenticated customers shall be allowed to resume an interrupted checkout session.
2. A saved checkout session shall belong only to the authenticated customer.
3. Only active and unexpired saved checkout sessions shall be eligible for resumption.
4. Completed checkout sessions shall not be resumable.
5. Cancelled checkout sessions shall not be resumable.
6. Expired checkout sessions shall not be resumable.
7. The most recently saved version of the checkout session shall be restored.
8. All restored checkout information shall be revalidated before customer access.
9. Restaurant operational status shall be revalidated.
10. Menu item availability shall be revalidated.
11. Product pricing shall be revalidated.
12. Taxes and service charges shall be recalculated where required.
13. Applied coupon validity shall be revalidated.
14. Wallet balance shall be revalidated before use.
15. Delivery address serviceability shall be revalidated.
16. Estimated delivery time shall be recalculated.
17. Selected delivery type shall be revalidated.
18. Selected payment method shall be revalidated.
19. Any invalid checkout information shall be automatically updated where possible.
20. Customers shall be notified of all checkout changes before proceeding.
21. Customer approval shall be required if checkout totals have changed.
22. Sensitive payment credentials shall never be restored from saved sessions.
23. A resumed checkout session shall become the active checkout session.
24. Previous inactive session versions shall be archived according to organizational policy.
25. Duplicate resume requests shall be processed idempotently.
26. Resume timestamp shall be generated by the system.
27. Successful resume operations shall be recorded in audit logs.
28. Failed resume operations shall also be recorded.
29. Resume operations shall maintain transactional consistency.
30. Resume functionality shall comply with organizational security policies.
31. Personally Identifiable Information (PII) shall remain protected during session restoration.
32. Organizational session retention policies shall be enforced.
33. Dynamic business rules shall always take precedence over saved data.
34. Operational monitoring events shall be generated for resume operations.
35. The system shall maintain complete data integrity throughout the session restoration process.

---

#### 3.5.22.7 Validations

1. Verify customer authentication.
2. Verify customer account status.
3. Verify saved checkout session validity.
4. Verify saved checkout session ownership.
5. Verify saved checkout session has not expired.
6. Verify saved checkout session has not already been completed.
7. Verify saved checkout session has not been cancelled.
8. Verify Shopping Cart Service availability.
9. Verify Checkout Service availability.
10. Verify Session Management Service availability.
11. Verify Restaurant Management Service availability.
12. Verify Menu Management Service availability.
13. Verify Pricing Service availability.
14. Verify Coupon Management Service availability.
15. Verify Wallet Management Service availability.
16. Verify Delivery Management Service availability.
17. Verify database connectivity.
18. Verify Audit Logging Service availability.
19. Verify checkout version consistency.
20. Verify menu item availability.
21. Verify latest pricing information.
22. Verify coupon validity.
23. Verify wallet balance.
24. Verify delivery address serviceability.
25. Verify selected payment method eligibility.
26. Verify successful restoration of eligible checkout data.
27. Verify audit event generation.
28. Verify transactional consistency before completing the resume operation.

#### 3.5.22.8 Main Flow

1. Customer logs in to the application.
2. Customer selects **Resume Checkout**.
3. System receives the resume request.
4. System validates the customer's authentication.
5. System validates the saved checkout session.
6. System verifies that the saved session belongs to the authenticated customer.
7. System verifies that the saved session has not expired.
8. System retrieves the saved checkout session.
9. System retrieves the associated shopping cart.
10. System restores the saved checkout information.
11. System revalidates restaurant operational status.
12. System revalidates menu item availability.
13. System revalidates product pricing.
14. System recalculates taxes and service charges where applicable.
15. System revalidates the applied coupon, where applicable.
16. System revalidates wallet balance, where applicable.
17. System revalidates the delivery address serviceability.
18. System recalculates the estimated delivery time.
19. System validates the selected payment method.
20. System identifies any changes since the session was saved.
21. System updates invalid or outdated checkout information where possible.
22. System creates a new active checkout session from the restored data.
23. System archives the previous saved session version according to organizational policy.
24. System records the resume timestamp.
25. System records the resume event in the audit logs.
26. System updates the checkout status as **Resumed**.
27. System displays the refreshed checkout summary.
28. Customer reviews any updated checkout information.
29. Customer continues the checkout process.
30. Resume Interrupted Checkout process completes successfully.

---

#### 3.5.22.9 Alternate Flows

**AF-1: Saved Session Expired**

1. Customer selects a saved checkout session.
2. System detects that the saved session has expired.
3. Session restoration is rejected.
4. Customer is informed that the saved session has expired.
5. Customer starts a new checkout process.

---

**AF-2: Restaurant No Longer Accepting Orders**

1. System revalidates restaurant availability.
2. Restaurant is currently unavailable.
3. Session restoration is completed with a warning.
4. Customer is informed that checkout cannot continue for the selected restaurant.
5. Customer modifies the shopping cart or chooses another restaurant.

---

**AF-3: Menu Item Unavailable**

1. System validates menu item availability.
2. One or more items are unavailable.
3. Unavailable items are identified.
4. Customer is prompted to update the shopping cart.
5. Checkout resumes after customer review.

---

**AF-4: Price Changed**

1. System detects updated menu pricing.
2. Checkout totals are recalculated.
3. Customer is shown the revised pricing.
4. Customer reviews the updated checkout summary.
5. Customer continues checkout after confirmation.

---

**AF-5: Coupon Expired**

1. System validates the applied coupon.
2. Coupon is no longer valid.
3. Coupon is removed automatically.
4. Checkout totals are recalculated.
5. Customer may apply another coupon.

---

**AF-6: Wallet Balance Changed**

1. System validates the customer's wallet balance.
2. Wallet balance is insufficient.
3. Wallet deduction is updated.
4. Remaining payable amount is recalculated.
5. Customer continues with the revised payment amount.

---

**AF-7: Duplicate Resume Request**

1. Multiple resume requests are received.
2. System detects duplicate requests.
3. Only one resume operation is processed.
4. Remaining requests receive the existing response.
5. Duplicate active checkout sessions are prevented.

---

**AF-8: Audit Logging Service Unavailable**

1. Resume operation completes successfully.
2. Audit Logging Service is temporarily unavailable.
3. Audit event is queued according to retry policies.
4. Customer workflow continues without interruption.

---

**AF-9: Saved Session Not Found**

1. Customer requests to resume a checkout.
2. System cannot locate the saved session.
3. Resume request is rejected.
4. Customer receives an appropriate notification.
5. Customer starts a new checkout process.

---

**AF-10: Internal System Error**

1. An unexpected application error occurs.
2. System records the exception.
3. Resume process is terminated safely.
4. Customer receives a generic error message.
5. Customer may retry the resume operation later.

#### 3.5.22.10 Postconditions

Upon successful completion of the Resume Interrupted Checkout process, the following postconditions shall be satisfied:

1. The interrupted checkout session shall be successfully restored.
2. A new active checkout session shall be created from the restored data.
3. The previous saved session shall remain archived according to organizational policy.
4. The shopping cart shall be restored successfully.
5. Restaurant availability shall be revalidated.
6. Menu item availability shall be revalidated.
7. Latest product pricing shall be applied.
8. Taxes and service charges shall be recalculated where applicable.
9. Coupon validity shall be revalidated.
10. Wallet balance shall be refreshed.
11. Delivery address serviceability shall be revalidated.
12. Estimated delivery time shall be recalculated.
13. Selected payment method shall be revalidated.
14. Any outdated checkout information shall be updated.
15. Resume timestamp shall be recorded.
16. Resume operation shall be recorded in the audit logs.
17. Checkout status shall be updated as **Resumed**.
18. Customer shall be able to continue the checkout process from the restored state.

---

#### 3.5.22.11 Success Response

Upon successful completion of the Resume Interrupted Checkout process, the system shall:

1. Display a confirmation that the checkout session has been successfully restored.
2. Display the updated checkout summary.
3. Display the latest product prices.
4. Display recalculated taxes and service charges where applicable.
5. Display updated coupon information where applicable.
6. Display updated wallet deduction where applicable.
7. Display the latest estimated delivery time.
8. Display updated payable amount.
9. Highlight any checkout information that has changed since the session was saved.
10. Restore the customer's shopping cart.
11. Activate the restored checkout session.
12. Allow the customer to continue the checkout process.

---

#### 3.5.22.12 Failure Response

If the Resume Interrupted Checkout process cannot be completed, the system shall:

1. Reject the resume request.
2. Display an appropriate validation or system error message.
3. Inform the customer if the saved session has expired.
4. Inform the customer if the saved session cannot be found.
5. Inform the customer if the restaurant is unavailable.
6. Inform the customer if menu items are unavailable.
7. Inform the customer if required backend services are unavailable.
8. Prevent restoration of incomplete or inconsistent checkout data.
9. Record failure details in application and audit logs.
10. Maintain transactional consistency.
11. Allow the customer to retry the resume operation where applicable.
12. Escalate unrecoverable failures according to organizational operational policies.

---

#### 3.5.22.13 Acceptance Criteria

The Resume Interrupted Checkout functionality shall be considered successfully implemented when all the following conditions are satisfied:

1. Only authenticated customers can resume saved checkout sessions.
2. Saved checkout session ownership is successfully verified.
3. Expired sessions cannot be resumed.
4. Completed sessions cannot be resumed.
5. Cancelled sessions cannot be resumed.
6. Shopping cart is restored successfully.
7. Restaurant availability is revalidated.
8. Menu item availability is revalidated.

11. Coupon validity is revalidated.
12. Wallet balance is revalidated.
13. Delivery address serviceability is revalidated.
14. Estimated delivery time is recalculated.
15. Payment method eligibility is revalidated.
16. Changed checkout information is clearly presented to the customer.
17. Resume operations are recorded in audit logs.
18. Transactional consistency is maintained.
19. Sensitive payment credentials are never restored.
20. Customer can successfully continue checkout after session restoration.

---

#### 3.5.22.14 Security Considerations

1. Only authenticated customers shall be permitted to resume checkout sessions.
2. Customer authorization shall be verified before restoring checkout data.
3. Saved Checkout Session IDs shall be validated before processing.
4. All communication shall occur over HTTPS with TLS encryption.
5. Saved checkout data shall remain encrypted at rest.
6. Sensitive payment credentials shall never be restored.
7. Personally Identifiable Information (PII) shall remain protected throughout the restoration process.
8. Role-based access control shall restrict access to saved checkout sessions.
9. All requests shall be protected against SQL Injection, Cross-Site Scripting (XSS), Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF), replay attacks, parameter tampering, and other injection attacks.
10. Unauthorized resume attempts shall be blocked and recorded for security auditing.

### 3.5.23 Validate Restaurant Availability

#### 3.5.23.1 Description

The Validate Restaurant Availability use case verifies whether the selected restaurant is currently eligible to accept and fulfill the customer's order before the checkout process proceeds. The system shall validate the restaurant's operational status, business hours, temporary closures, holiday schedules, delivery service availability, order acceptance capacity, serviceable delivery area, and other operational constraints.

The validation shall ensure that customers cannot place orders with restaurants that are temporarily closed, permanently inactive, outside business hours, not accepting new orders, outside the customer's delivery location, or otherwise unavailable for order fulfillment.

If the restaurant is unavailable, the system shall clearly communicate the reason, prevent checkout continuation, and where applicable, provide alternative actions such as scheduling the order, modifying the cart, or selecting another restaurant.

Restaurant availability validation shall be performed whenever required during the checkout lifecycle to ensure that customers always receive the latest operational status before order confirmation.

All validation activities shall be securely logged for auditing, operational monitoring, troubleshooting, and business analytics.

---

#### 3.5.23.2 Actors

**Primary Actor**

- Customer

**Supporting Actors**

- Checkout Service
- Restaurant Management Service
- Menu Management Service
- Delivery Management Service
- Geographic Service
- Configuration Management Service
- Notification Service
- Audit Logging Service
- Database Management System

---

#### 3.5.23.3 Preconditions

Before validating restaurant availability, the following conditions shall be satisfied:

1. Customer shall have an active authenticated session.
2. Customer shall have an active checkout session.
3. Shopping cart shall contain at least one menu item.
4. A restaurant shall be selected.
5. Restaurant identifier shall be valid.
6. Customer delivery address shall be available.
7. Checkout Service shall be operational.
8. Restaurant Management Service shall be operational.
9. Delivery Management Service shall be operational.
10. Geographic Service shall be operational.
11. Menu Management Service shall be operational.
12. Configuration Management Service shall be operational.
13. Audit Logging Service shall be operational.
14. Database connectivity shall be available.
15. Restaurant master data shall be accessible.
16. Restaurant operational schedule shall be available.
17. Customer location information shall be available.
18. All dependent backend services shall be operational.

---

#### 3.5.23.4 Trigger

The use case is initiated when the customer proceeds with checkout or whenever the system needs to verify the latest operational availability of the selected restaurant before allowing the checkout process to continue.

---

#### 3.5.23.5 Input Fields

| Field Name | Description | Mandatory | Validation |
|------------|-------------|-----------|------------|
| Restaurant ID | Selected restaurant identifier | Yes | Must exist |
| Customer ID | Authenticated customer identifier | Yes | Must be valid |
| Checkout Session ID | Active checkout session | Yes | Must exist |
| Shopping Cart ID | Customer shopping cart identifier | Yes | Must exist |
| Delivery Address ID | Selected delivery address | Yes | Must exist |
| Geographic Coordinates | Customer delivery location | Yes | Valid latitude and longitude |
| Validation Request ID | Unique validation request identifier | Yes | System generated |
| Request Timestamp | Validation request timestamp | Yes | System generated |
| Source Channel | Mobile App, Web, Tablet | Yes | Supported channel |
| Session Token | Authenticated session token | Yes | Must be valid |

#### 3.5.23.6 Business Rules

1. Restaurant availability validation shall be performed before order confirmation.
2. Only active restaurants shall be allowed to receive orders.
3. Restaurants marked as permanently inactive shall not accept orders.
4. Restaurants marked as temporarily closed shall not accept orders.
5. Restaurant business hours shall be validated.
6. Holiday schedules shall be validated.
7. Temporary maintenance schedules shall be validated.
8. Restaurant order acceptance status shall be verified.
9. Restaurant maximum order capacity shall be verified.
10. Restaurant concurrent order limit shall be validated.
11. Delivery service availability shall be validated.
12. Customer delivery location shall fall within the restaurant's serviceable delivery area.
13. Delivery radius restrictions shall be enforced.
14. Restaurant-specific delivery restrictions shall be enforced.
15. Menu availability validation shall occur only after successful restaurant availability validation.
16. Restaurants suspended due to compliance or operational reasons shall not accept orders.
17. Restaurants blocked by administrative action shall not accept orders.
18. Restaurant operational configuration shall always use the latest published data.
19. Dynamic operational changes shall immediately affect checkout eligibility.
20. Customers shall receive a meaningful reason when restaurant validation fails.
21. Estimated delivery time shall only be calculated for available restaurants.
22. Restaurant availability shall be revalidated before final order confirmation.
23. Duplicate validation requests shall be processed idempotently.
24. Validation results shall not permanently reserve restaurant capacity.
25. Validation shall not create an order record.
26. Validation shall not reserve menu inventory.
27. Successful validation events shall be recorded in audit logs.
28. Failed validation events shall also be recorded.
29. Validation processing shall maintain transactional consistency.
30. Restaurant availability rules shall comply with organizational operational policies.
31. Personally Identifiable Information (PII) shall remain protected during validation.
32. Validation responses shall use the latest operational data.
33. System-generated validation timestamps shall be recorded.
34. Operational monitoring events shall be generated for validation activities.
35. Data integrity shall be maintained throughout the validation process.

---

#### 3.5.23.7 Validations

1. Verify customer authentication.
2. Verify active checkout session.
3. Verify restaurant identifier validity.
4. Verify restaurant exists.
5. Verify restaurant is active.
6. Verify restaurant is not permanently inactive.
7. Verify restaurant is not temporarily closed.
8. Verify restaurant business hours.
9. Verify holiday schedule.
10. Verify maintenance schedule.
11. Verify restaurant is currently accepting orders.
12. Verify restaurant order capacity.
13. Verify concurrent order limit.
14. Verify delivery service availability.
15. Verify customer delivery address.
16. Verify customer delivery location is serviceable.
17. Verify delivery radius restrictions.
18. Verify Checkout Service availability.
19. Verify Restaurant Management Service availability.
20. Verify Delivery Management Service availability.
21. Verify Geographic Service availability.
22. Verify Menu Management Service availability.
23. Verify Configuration Management Service availability.
24. Verify database connectivity.
25. Verify Audit Logging Service availability.
26. Verify validation response consistency.
27. Verify audit event generation.
28. Verify transactional consistency before completing restaurant availability validation.

#### 3.5.23.8 Main Flow

1. Customer proceeds with the checkout process.
2. System initiates restaurant availability validation.
3. System validates the customer's active session.
4. System validates the active checkout session.
5. System retrieves the selected restaurant details.
6. System verifies that the restaurant exists.
7. System verifies that the restaurant is active.
8. System verifies that the restaurant is not permanently inactive.
9. System verifies that the restaurant is not temporarily closed.
10. System validates the restaurant's business hours.
11. System validates the restaurant's holiday schedule.
12. System validates any temporary maintenance schedule.
13. System verifies that the restaurant is currently accepting new orders.
14. System verifies the restaurant's current operational status.
15. System verifies available order processing capacity.
16. System validates concurrent order limits.
17. System retrieves the customer's selected delivery address.
18. System validates the delivery address serviceability.
19. System verifies that the delivery location falls within the restaurant's serviceable area.
20. System validates applicable delivery radius restrictions.
21. System verifies delivery partner availability for the selected location.
22. System confirms that restaurant delivery service is available.
23. System generates the latest restaurant availability status.
24. System records the validation timestamp.
25. System records the validation event in the audit logs.
26. System marks the restaurant as available for checkout.
27. System allows the checkout process to continue.
28. Customer proceeds to the next checkout validation.
29. System maintains the validated availability status until the next mandatory revalidation.
30. Validate Restaurant Availability process completes successfully.

---

#### 3.5.23.9 Alternate Flows

**AF-1: Restaurant Permanently Inactive**

1. System validates restaurant status.
2. Restaurant is marked as permanently inactive.
3. Validation fails.
4. Customer is informed that the restaurant is unavailable.
5. Customer selects another restaurant.

---

**AF-2: Restaurant Temporarily Closed**

1. System validates restaurant operational status.
2. Restaurant is temporarily closed.
3. Validation is rejected.
4. Customer is informed of the temporary closure.
5. Customer may try again later or choose another restaurant.

---

**AF-3: Outside Business Hours**

1. System validates restaurant business hours.
2. Current time falls outside operating hours.
3. Checkout continuation is blocked.
4. Customer is informed of the restaurant's operating hours.
5. Customer may schedule the order if supported.

---

**AF-4: Restaurant Not Accepting Orders**

1. System verifies order acceptance status.
2. Restaurant is not accepting new orders.
3. Validation fails.
4. Customer receives an appropriate notification.
5. Customer selects another restaurant or retries later.

---

**AF-5: Delivery Location Not Serviceable**

1. System validates the delivery address.
2. Delivery location falls outside the restaurant's service area.
3. Validation fails.
4. Customer is informed that delivery is unavailable for the selected address.
5. Customer selects another address or another restaurant.

---

**AF-6: Restaurant Capacity Reached**

1. System verifies current order capacity.
2. Maximum order capacity has been reached.
3. Validation is rejected.
4. Customer is notified that the restaurant is temporarily busy.
5. Customer retries later or selects another restaurant.

---

**AF-7: Duplicate Validation Request**

1. Multiple validation requests are received.
2. System detects duplicate requests.
3. Only one validation process is executed.
4. Remaining requests receive the same validation response.
5. Duplicate processing is prevented.

---

**AF-8: Audit Logging Service Unavailable**

1. Restaurant validation succeeds.
2. Audit Logging Service is temporarily unavailable.
3. Validation event is queued according to retry policies.
4. Checkout continues without interruption.

---

**AF-9: Restaurant Data Unavailable**

1. System attempts to retrieve restaurant information.
2. Restaurant data cannot be retrieved.
3. Validation fails.
4. Customer receives a temporary system message.
5. Customer may retry later.

---

**AF-10: Internal System Error**

1. An unexpected application error occurs.
2. System records the exception.
3. Validation process is safely terminated.
4. Customer receives a generic error message.
5. Customer may retry the checkout process later.

Upon successful completion of the Validate Restaurant Availability process, the following postconditions shall be satisfied:

1. Restaurant availability shall be successfully validated.
2. Restaurant operational status shall be confirmed.
3. Restaurant business hours shall be verified.

17. Transactional consistency shall be maintained.
18. Restaurant availability shall remain valid until the next mandatory revalidation event.

Upon successful completion of the Validate Restaurant Availability process, the system shall:

2. Display the restaurant's current operational status.
3. Confirm that the restaurant is accepting orders.
4. Confirm that the delivery location is serviceable.
5. Display the latest estimated delivery availability.

9. Maintain transactional consistency.
10. Continue to the next checkout validation process.

12. Display the updated checkout summary where applicable.

If the Validate Restaurant Availability process cannot be completed, the system shall:

2. Display an appropriate validation or system error message.

4. Inform the customer if the restaurant is outside business hours.
5. Inform the customer if the restaurant is not accepting orders.
6. Inform the customer if the delivery location is outside the serviceable area.
7. Inform the customer if restaurant capacity has been exceeded.
8. Prevent order confirmation for unavailable restaurants.

10. Maintain transactional consistency.
11. Allow the customer to retry validation where applicable.
12. Escalate unrecoverable failures according to organizational operational policies.

2. Restaurant active status is verified.

4. Temporarily closed restaurants cannot accept orders.

6. Holiday schedules are validated.

### 3.5.24 Validate Delivery Service Availability

#### 3.5.24.1 Description

The Validate Delivery Service Availability use case verifies whether delivery service is currently available for the customer's selected delivery address before allowing the checkout process to continue. The system shall validate delivery partner availability, delivery zone coverage, serviceable location, operational hours, weather-related restrictions, high-demand conditions, temporary delivery suspensions, and other operational constraints that may impact successful order fulfillment.

The validation ensures that orders are accepted only when the delivery infrastructure is capable of fulfilling the order within the organization's service standards. The system shall also evaluate delivery network capacity, estimated delivery feasibility, and regional delivery restrictions.

If delivery service is unavailable, the system shall prevent checkout continuation, clearly communicate the reason for the failure, and where applicable, provide alternative options such as scheduling delivery, changing the delivery address, selecting pickup (if supported), or retrying later.

Delivery service availability validation shall be performed whenever required during the checkout lifecycle, especially before order confirmation, to ensure that customers always receive the latest operational delivery status.

All validation activities shall be securely recorded for auditing, operational monitoring, troubleshooting, analytics, capacity planning, and compliance reporting.

---

#### 3.5.24.2 Actors

**Primary Actor**

- Customer

**Supporting Actors**

- Checkout Service
- Delivery Management Service
- Delivery Partner Management Service
- Geographic Service
- Capacity Management Service
- Restaurant Management Service
- Notification Service
- Audit Logging Service
- Database Management System

---

#### 3.5.24.3 Preconditions

Before validating delivery service availability, the following conditions shall be satisfied:

1. Customer shall have an active authenticated session.
2. Customer shall have an active checkout session.
3. Shopping cart shall contain at least one menu item.
4. A restaurant shall already be validated as available.
5. A delivery address shall be selected.
6. Delivery address shall be valid.
7. Checkout Service shall be operational.
8. Delivery Management Service shall be operational.
9. Delivery Partner Management Service shall be operational.
10. Geographic Service shall be operational.
11. Capacity Management Service shall be operational.
12. Restaurant Management Service shall be operational.
13. Audit Logging Service shall be operational.
14. Database connectivity shall be available.
15. Delivery network configuration shall be accessible.
16. Delivery partner availability data shall be accessible.
17. Customer location information shall be available.
18. All dependent backend services shall be operational.

---

#### 3.5.24.4 Trigger

The use case is initiated when the customer proceeds with checkout or whenever the system needs to verify that delivery service is currently available for the selected delivery address before allowing checkout to continue.

---

#### 3.5.24.5 Input Fields

| Field Name | Description | Mandatory | Validation |
|------------|-------------|-----------|------------|
| Delivery Address ID | Selected delivery address identifier | Yes | Must exist |
| Customer ID | Authenticated customer identifier | Yes | Must be valid |
| Checkout Session ID | Active checkout session identifier | Yes | Must exist |
| Restaurant ID | Selected restaurant identifier | Yes | Must exist |
| Geographic Coordinates | Delivery location coordinates | Yes | Valid latitude and longitude |
| Delivery Zone ID | Delivery service zone identifier | Yes | Must exist |
| Validation Request ID | Unique validation request identifier | Yes | System generated |
| Request Timestamp | Validation request timestamp | Yes | System generated |
| Source Channel | Mobile App, Web, Tablet | Yes | Supported channel |
| Session Token | Authenticated session token | Yes | Must be valid |

#### 3.5.24.6 Business Rules

1. Delivery service availability shall be validated before order confirmation.
2. Delivery shall be available only within supported delivery zones.
3. The selected delivery address shall fall within a serviceable delivery area.
4. Delivery partner availability shall be verified in real time.
5. Delivery partner capacity shall be validated before accepting an order.
6. Delivery operational hours shall be validated.
7. Regional delivery restrictions shall be enforced.
8. Temporary delivery suspensions shall prevent checkout continuation.
9. Weather-related delivery restrictions shall be considered where applicable.
10. High-demand operational restrictions shall be enforced.
11. Delivery network capacity shall be validated.
12. Estimated delivery feasibility shall be calculated.
13. Delivery radius limitations shall be enforced.
14. Restaurant delivery support shall be verified.
15. Delivery type shall be validated against operational capabilities.
16. Scheduled delivery requests shall be validated separately from immediate deliveries.
17. The latest delivery configuration shall always be used.
18. Dynamic delivery availability changes shall immediately affect checkout eligibility.
19. Customers shall receive meaningful failure messages when delivery validation fails.
20. Delivery availability shall be revalidated before final order confirmation.
21. Duplicate validation requests shall be processed idempotently.
22. Validation shall not reserve a delivery partner.
23. Validation shall not reserve delivery capacity permanently.
24. Validation shall not create a delivery assignment.
25. Successful validation events shall be recorded in audit logs.
26. Failed validation events shall also be recorded.
27. Validation processing shall maintain transactional consistency.
28. Delivery availability rules shall comply with organizational operational policies.
29. Personally Identifiable Information (PII) shall remain protected during validation.
30. Validation responses shall always use the latest operational data.
31. System-generated validation timestamps shall be recorded.
32. Operational monitoring events shall be generated for delivery validation.
33. Validation shall support horizontal scalability for peak traffic.
34. Delivery availability decisions shall be deterministic for identical request parameters.
35. Data integrity shall be maintained throughout the validation process.

1. Verify customer authentication.
2. Verify active checkout session.
3. Verify restaurant has already passed availability validation.
4. Verify delivery address validity.
5. Verify delivery address exists.
6. Verify delivery zone validity.
7. Verify geographic coordinates.
8. Verify delivery serviceability.
9. Verify delivery partner availability.
10. Verify delivery operational hours.
11. Verify delivery network capacity.

13. Verify delivery radius eligibility.

15. Verify high-demand operational restrictions.
16. Verify restaurant delivery support.

18. Verify scheduled delivery eligibility where applicable.
19. Verify Checkout Service availability.
20. Verify Delivery Management Service availability.
21. Verify Delivery Partner Management Service availability.
22. Verify Geographic Service availability.
23. Verify Capacity Management Service availability.
24. Verify Restaurant Management Service availability.

2. System initiates delivery service availability validation.
3. System validates the customer's authenticated session.

27. System marks delivery service as available.
28. System allows checkout to proceed to the next validation stage.

30. Validate Delivery Service Availability process completes successfully.

### 3.5.25 Generate Order Request

#### 3.5.25.1 Description

The Generate Order Request use case creates a validated order request after all checkout validations have been successfully completed and before payment processing or final order creation begins. The system shall consolidate all verified checkout information, including customer details, restaurant information, shopping cart items, delivery details, pricing breakdown, taxes, discounts, coupons, wallet deductions, delivery charges, and payment preferences into a single immutable order request.

The generated order request serves as the canonical representation of the customer's purchase intent and acts as the input for downstream services such as Payment Processing, Order Management, Inventory Validation, Restaurant Order Acceptance, and Delivery Assignment. The order request itself shall not represent a confirmed order and shall not initiate payment authorization, inventory reservation, or restaurant notification.

Before generating the order request, the system shall ensure that all prerequisite validations have been completed successfully and that no critical checkout information has changed since the final validation. The generated request shall contain a unique identifier, version information, timestamps, and integrity metadata to support traceability, idempotency, auditing, and distributed transaction processing.

All order request generation activities shall be securely logged for auditing, compliance, monitoring, troubleshooting, analytics, and operational reporting.

---

#### 3.5.25.2 Actors

**Primary Actor**

- Customer

**Supporting Actors**

- Checkout Service
- Order Management Service
- Shopping Cart Service
- Pricing Service
- Restaurant Management Service
- Payment Service
- Coupon Management Service
- Wallet Management Service
- Delivery Management Service
- Inventory Management Service
- Notification Service
- Audit Logging Service
- Database Management System

---

#### 3.5.25.3 Preconditions

Before generating an order request, the following conditions shall be satisfied:

1. Customer shall have an authenticated session.
2. Customer shall have an active checkout session.
3. Shopping cart shall contain at least one valid item.
4. Restaurant availability shall already be validated.
5. Delivery service availability shall already be validated.
6. Checkout information shall be successfully validated.
7. Final payable amount shall be calculated.
8. Delivery address shall be selected.
9. Payment method shall be selected.
10. Customer shall have accepted the Terms and Conditions.
11. Checkout Service shall be operational.
12. Order Management Service shall be operational.
13. Pricing Service shall be operational.
14. Inventory Management Service shall be operational.
15. Audit Logging Service shall be operational.
16. Database connectivity shall be available.
17. No conflicting checkout modifications shall exist.
18. All dependent backend services shall be operational.

---

#### 3.5.25.4 Trigger

The use case is initiated when the customer clicks **Proceed to Payment**, **Place Order**, or another equivalent action after successfully completing all required checkout validations.

---

#### 3.5.25.5 Input Fields

| Field Name | Description | Mandatory | Validation |
|------------|-------------|-----------|------------|
| Checkout Session ID | Active checkout session identifier | Yes | Must exist |
| Customer ID | Authenticated customer identifier | Yes | Must be valid |
| Shopping Cart ID | Shopping cart identifier | Yes | Must exist |
| Restaurant ID | Selected restaurant identifier | Yes | Must exist |
| Delivery Address ID | Selected delivery address identifier | Yes | Must exist |
| Payment Method ID | Selected payment method | Yes | Supported payment method |
| Final Payable Amount | Final amount after all calculations | Yes | Must match calculated amount |
| Request Timestamp | Order request generation timestamp | Yes | System generated |
| Request ID | Unique order request identifier | Yes | System generated |
| Session Token | Authenticated session token | Yes | Must be valid |

#### 3.5.25.6 Business Rules

1. An order request shall be generated only after all mandatory checkout validations are completed successfully.
2. Only authenticated customers shall be permitted to generate an order request.
3. The order request shall be generated from the latest validated checkout session.
4. The checkout session shall belong to the authenticated customer.
5. The shopping cart shall contain at least one valid menu item.
6. Restaurant availability shall be successfully validated before generating the order request.
7. Delivery service availability shall be successfully validated before generating the order request.
8. The final payable amount shall exactly match the latest pricing calculation.
9. Applied coupons shall be revalidated before generating the order request.
10. Wallet deductions shall reflect the latest available wallet balance.
11. Delivery charges shall use the latest applicable configuration.
12. Taxes and service charges shall be recalculated using the latest tax rules.
13. Customer delivery address shall be valid and serviceable.
14. Selected payment method shall remain eligible.
15. Terms and Conditions acceptance shall be verified.
16. The order request shall contain a globally unique Order Request ID.
17. The order request shall contain a unique version number.
18. A system-generated request timestamp shall be recorded.
19. The generated order request shall be immutable after creation.
20. Duplicate order request generation shall be processed idempotently.
21. An order request shall not create an order record.
22. An order request shall not authorize payment.
23. An order request shall not reserve inventory.
24. An order request shall not assign a delivery partner.
25. An order request shall not notify the restaurant.
26. All monetary values shall use standardized currency precision.
27. Personally Identifiable Information (PII) shall be protected throughout request generation.
28. Sensitive payment credentials shall never be stored within the order request.
29. Successful order request generation shall be recorded in audit logs.
30. Failed order request generation shall also be recorded.
31. Order request generation shall maintain transactional consistency.
32. The generated request shall be available to downstream services.
33. Operational monitoring events shall be generated.
34. Organizational data retention policies shall apply to generated requests.
35. Data integrity shall be maintained throughout the request generation process.

---

#### 3.5.25.7 Validations

1. Verify customer authentication.
2. Verify active checkout session.
3. Verify checkout session ownership.
4. Verify shopping cart contains valid items.
5. Verify restaurant availability validation status.
6. Verify delivery service availability validation status.
7. Verify final pricing calculation.
8. Verify taxes and service charges.
9. Verify applied coupon validity.
10. Verify wallet deduction.
11. Verify delivery charges.
12. Verify delivery address validity.
13. Verify delivery address serviceability.
14. Verify selected payment method eligibility.
15. Verify Terms and Conditions acceptance.
16. Verify Checkout Service availability.
17. Verify Order Management Service availability.
18. Verify Pricing Service availability.
19. Verify Inventory Management Service availability.
20. Verify Payment Service availability.
21. Verify Coupon Management Service availability.
22. Verify Wallet Management Service availability.
23. Verify Delivery Management Service availability.
24. Verify database connectivity.
25. Verify Audit Logging Service availability.
26. Verify generated Order Request ID uniqueness.
27. Verify successful audit event generation.
28. Verify transactional consistency before completing order request generation.

#### 3.5.25.10 Postconditions

Upon successful completion of the Generate Order Request process, the following postconditions shall be satisfied:

1. A unique Order Request shall be successfully generated.
2. The generated Order Request shall contain the latest validated checkout information.
3. The generated Order Request shall be immutable.
4. The generated Order Request shall be associated with the active checkout session.
5. The Order Request ID shall be permanently recorded.
6. The Order Request version shall be recorded.
7. The request generation timestamp shall be recorded.
8. Pricing information shall reflect the latest validated calculations.
9. Delivery information shall reflect the latest validated data.
10. Payment preferences shall be associated with the generated Order Request.
11. Integrity metadata shall be generated and stored.
12. The generated Order Request shall be securely persisted.
13. Order Request status shall be updated as **Generated**.
14. The generated Order Request shall be available for downstream processing.
15. Successful generation shall be recorded in audit logs.
16. Transactional consistency shall be maintained.
17. No payment authorization shall occur.
18. No order record shall be created until subsequent workflow stages are completed.

---

#### 3.5.25.11 Success Response

Upon successful completion of the Generate Order Request process, the system shall:

1. Confirm successful Order Request generation.
2. Display the generated Order Request reference number where applicable.
3. Confirm that checkout validation has completed successfully.
4. Confirm that the latest pricing has been applied.
5. Confirm that delivery details have been validated.
6. Confirm that payment information has been accepted for processing.
7. Associate the generated Order Request with the checkout session.
8. Record the successful generation event.
9. Maintain transactional consistency.
10. Make the Order Request available to downstream services.
11. Redirect the customer to the payment processing workflow.
12. Preserve all validated checkout information.

---

#### 3.5.25.12 Failure Response

If the Generate Order Request process cannot be completed, the system shall:

1. Reject the request generation process.
2. Display an appropriate validation or system error message.
3. Inform the customer if checkout validation has become invalid.
4. Inform the customer if restaurant or delivery validation has failed.
5. Inform the customer if pricing has changed.
6. Inform the customer if the selected payment method is no longer valid.
7. Prevent creation of incomplete Order Requests.
8. Roll back partially completed operations where applicable.
9. Record failure details in application and audit logs.
10. Maintain transactional consistency.
11. Allow the customer to retry the operation where applicable.
12. Escalate unrecoverable failures according to organizational operational policies.

---

#### 3.5.25.13 Acceptance Criteria

The Generate Order Request functionality shall be considered successfully implemented when all the following conditions are satisfied:

1. Only authenticated customers can generate an Order Request.
2. Checkout session ownership is successfully verified.
3. Latest validated checkout data is used.
4. Restaurant availability validation is confirmed.
5. Delivery service availability validation is confirmed.
6. Final payable amount matches the latest calculation.
7. Applied coupons are successfully validated.
8. Wallet deductions are successfully validated.
9. Delivery address validity is confirmed.
10. Payment method eligibility is confirmed.
11. A globally unique Order Request ID is generated.
12. Order Request version is generated.
13. Generated Order Request is immutable.
14. Duplicate request generation is handled safely.
15. Order Request is successfully persisted.
16. Successful generation is recorded in audit logs.
17. Transactional consistency is maintained.
18. No payment authorization occurs during request generation.
19. No order record is created during request generation.
20. Generated Order Request is available for downstream services.

---

#### 3.5.25.14 Security Considerations

1. Only authenticated customers shall be permitted to generate an Order Request.
2. Customer authorization shall be verified before processing.
3. Checkout Session IDs shall be validated before request generation.
4. All communications shall occur over HTTPS with TLS encryption.
5. Generated Order Requests shall be securely stored using approved organizational security standards.
6. Sensitive payment credentials shall never be stored within the generated Order Request.
7. Personally Identifiable Information (PII) shall be protected according to organizational privacy policies.
8. Role-based access control shall restrict access to generated Order Requests.
9. All requests shall be protected against SQL Injection, Cross-Site Scripting (XSS), Cross-Site Request Forgery (CSRF), replay attacks, parameter tampering, and other injection attacks.
10. Unauthorized Order Request generation attempts shall be rejected and recorded for security auditing.

---

#### 3.5.25.15 Audit Log Requirements

The system shall record the following audit events during the Generate Order Request process:

1. Order Request generation timestamp.
2. Customer ID.
3. Checkout Session ID.
4. Order Request ID.
5. Shopping Cart ID.
6. Restaurant ID.
7. Payment Method ID.
8. Order Request version.
9. Request generation status.
10. Customer IP Address.
11. Device Type.
12. Browser or Application Version.
13. Processing duration.
14. Failure reason, where applicable.
15. Audit logs shall be immutable and retained according to the organization's retention policy.

---

#### 3.5.25.16 Assumptions

The Generate Order Request functionality is implemented based on the following assumptions:

1. Customer authentication has already been completed.
2. An active checkout session exists.
3. All prerequisite checkout validations have completed successfully.
4. Restaurant availability has been validated.
5. Delivery service availability has been validated.
6. Checkout Service is operational.
7. Order Management Service is operational.
8. Database connectivity is available.
9. Audit Logging Service is operational.
10. All dependent backend services are operational.

---

The Generate Order Request functionality depends on the successful availability of the following components and services:

2. Checkout Service

4. Shopping Cart Service

6. Restaurant Management Service
7. Payment Service
